```text
The Min-K++ output doesn't show which reference model was used. The signal only has `s2_minkpp` and `s2_minkpp_raw` scores. Let me check the code to see if D016 was implemented (using d000 model as reference instead of raw base model).

Actually, looking at the data more carefully: the Min-K++ signal here appears to be a single score per item without a delta from a reference model. D016 said "Min-K++ delta = target - reference 的 log-likelihood 差", meaning the signal should be computed as a difference between the target model and a reference model. 

But the output just shows `s2_minkpp` without any reference model field, which suggests either the standard Min-K++ approach was used (just the target model's perplexity) or D016's reference model implementation hasn't been applied yet. D016 mentioned needing a new `--ref-adapter-path` parameter, which may not have been integrated into the signal computation yet. The exp_002_signals_05B was completed at 14, so I should check if that's when this signal was generated.

Actually, looking at the timestamps more carefully — the signal computation ran after D016 was made, but the Min-K++ AUC came back essentially random (0.46-0.47), which could mean either D016 wasn't implemented in the code or the reference model setup still isn't working properly at this scale. I need to look at the compute_signals.py file to see if the `--ref-adapter-path` parameter was actually added.
```

````text
D016 IS correctly implemented in `run_batch.py`:

Line 57-59:
```python
def ref_checkpoint_path(model_name: str) -> str:
    """D016: reference model = 0% dosage fine-tuned model."""
    return checkpoint_path(model_name, "d000")
```

Line 88: `ref_ckpt = ref_checkpoint_path(model_name)` 
Line 96: `"--ref-model-path", ref_ckpt`

So the reference model for Min-K++ is the d000 (0% dosage) fine-tuned model, exactly as D016 requires. 

The checkpoint path function constructs the full path to the LoRA adapter by combining the model name, dosage level, and seed. For d000, this resolves to the checkpoint directory I already confirmed exists in the registry at `/root/autodl-tmp/exp001/checkpoints/Qwen2.5-0.5B_d000_s42/`.

Now I'm reviewing the exp002 signal pipeline and confirming that all 9 task combinations are present with their signal files, the D016 compliance is correctly implemented with the d000 model as reference, and noting that S3 ConStat data is expectedly missing since it requires paraphrase data that isn't available yet.

The Min-K++ results are showing random performance around 0.46-0.47 AUC even with the proper d000 reference model, which suggests the method struggles at the 0.5B scale—possibly due to insufficient model capacity, ineffective z-score normalization at this scale, or fundamental limitations when applied to LoRA fine-tuning that only modifies low-rank parameters. This negative result actually supports the paper's argument that individual signals alone are insufficient for contamination detection.

The worker pipeline has completed successfully with verified signal outputs, proper D016 implementation, and data ready for analysis.
````

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent scientific Reviewer for the `contamcal-irt-calibration` project. My role is to review completed worker tasks by independently verifying experiment conclusions, checking GPU resource compliance, and reporting findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I received 6 sequential `worker_done` notifications and reviewed each one:
   - GPU status check (worker_ops_gpu-status-check)
   - exp-008 signal survival code worker failure (worker_code_exp-008-signal-survival)

   - Local CPU restart (worker_ops_local_cpu_restart_p0c) — **interrupted before posting review**

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **4PL+γ IRT model**: Item Response Theory model separating contamination effect (γ) from true ability (θ)
   - **Dose-response curve**: Testing at d000 (0%), d005 (5%), d025 (25%), d100 (100%) contamination dosages
   - **Three model scales**: Qwen2.5-0.5B, 1.5B, 3B with LoRA SFT
   - **Four contamination signals**: S1 Embedding, S2 Min-K++, S3 ConStat, S4 Self-Critique
   - **D017 aligned eval format**: "The answer is" prefix for MC evaluation
   - **D016 Min-K++ reference model**: 0% dosage fine-tuned model (not raw base)
   - **D020**: Reduced LoRA hyperparams (lr=5e-5, alpha=16) to fix 3B CoT catastrophic forgetting
   - **D021**: exp-008 methodological fix — d000/d100 within-model AUC is label artifact; need cross-model comparison
   - **Constraints C001-C003**: GPU process conflict prevention, nvidia-smi over resource_usage tool, kill processes on failed experiments
   - **Project target**: EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25 submission
   - **Server**: cqa1-21977, 6× RTX 4090 D 24GB, all allocated to project

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Registry (registry.yaml)**: 73 total experiments. Key RUNNING: exp_001_sft_3B_d000_v2 (GPU 5), exp_001_eval_05B_d025_aligned_v2 (GPU 5), exp_001_eval_05B_d100_aligned_v4 (GPU 2), exp_005_noise_prop_v3 (local), exp_004_sim_sweep_v2 (local), exp_001_sft_d025_15B (GPU 0), exp_001_eval_15B_aligned_batch (GPU 1)
   - **report.md**: Handover snapshot showing 8 RUNNING, 12+ PREPARING, 18 failed alerts

4. Errors and fixes:

```json
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

quick test\\n    python run_exp006.py                                         # full run\\n\\nDependencies: cmdstanpy, numpy, scipy\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport logging\\nimport os\\nimport sys\\nimport time\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\\nfrom run_irt_calibration import (\\n    load_eval_results,\\n    load_exposure_scores,\\n    build_exposure_matrix,\\n    run_benchmark_fit,\\n    STAN_MODELS,\\n)\\nfrom baselines import run_all_baselines\\nfrom evaluate_calibration import (\\n    evaluate_method,\\n    format_text_table,\\n    format_latex_table,\\n)\\nfrom joint_fit import run_joint_fit\\n\\nlog = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n# D018: 3B GSM8K excluded from dose-response analysis\\nD018_EXCLUDE = [(\\\"3B\\\", \\\"gsm8k\\\")]\\n\\n\\ndef should_exclude_dose_response(model_name, benchmark, rules=D018_EXCLUDE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Check D018 exclusion rules for dose-response analysis.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    for pattern, bench in rules:\\n        if pattern in model_name and benchmark == bench:\\n            return True\\n    return False\\n\\n\\ndef compute_ground_truth(responses, dosages):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Ground truth = accuracy of dosage-0 models (uncontaminated).\\n\\n    For dosage-0 models, raw accuracy IS the clean accuracy.\\n    For dosage>0 models, we use the mean d=0 accuracy as the\\n    counterfactual reference (what they would score without contamination).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    raw_acc = responses.mean(axis=1)\\n    d0_mask = dosages == 0\\n\\n    if d0_mask.sum() > 0:\\n        gt_acc_d0 = float(raw_acc[d0_mask].mean())\\n    else:\\n        gt_acc_d0 = float(raw_acc.mean())\\n\\n    # dosage-0 models: their own raw accuracy\\n    # dosage>0 models: d=0 mean as counterfactual reference\\n    ground_truth = np.full(len(dosages), gt_acc_d0)\\n    ground_truth[d0_mask] = raw_acc[d0_mask]\\n\\n    return ground_truth\\n\\n\\ndef run_pipeline(args):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run full exp-006 pipeline.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.4}}"}

quick test\\n    python run_exp006.py                                         # full run\\n\\nDependencies: cmdstanpy, numpy, scipy\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport logging\\nimport os\\nimport sys\\nimport time\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\\nfrom run_irt_calibration import (\\n    load_eval_results,\\n    load_exposure_scores,\\n    build_exposure_matrix,\\n    run_benchmark_fit,\\n    STAN_MODELS,\\n)\\nfrom baselines import run_all_baselines\\nfrom evaluate_calibration import (\\n    evaluate_method,\\n    format_text_table,\\n    format_latex_table,\\n)\\nfrom joint_fit import run_joint_fit\\n\\nlog = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n# D018: 3B GSM8K excluded from dose-response analysis\\nD018_EXCLUDE = [(\\\"3B\\\", \\\"gsm8k\\\")]\\n\\n\\ndef should_exclude_dose_response(model_name, benchmark, rules=D018_EXCLUDE):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Check D018 exclusion rules for dose-response analysis.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    for pattern, bench in rules:\\n        if pattern in model_name and benchmark == bench:\\n            return True\\n    return False\\n\\n\\ndef compute_ground_truth(responses, dosages):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Ground truth = accuracy of dosage-0 models (uncontaminated).\\n\\n    For dosage-0 models, raw accuracy IS the clean accuracy.\\n    For dosage>0 models, we use the mean d=0 accuracy as the\\n    counterfactual reference (what they would score without contamination).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    raw_acc = responses.mean(axis=1)\\n    d0_mask = dosages == 0\\n\\n    if d0_mask.sum() > 0:\\n        gt_acc_d0 = float(raw_acc[d0_mask].mean())\\n    else:\\n        gt_acc_d0 = float(raw_acc.mean())\\n\\n    # dosage-0 models: their own raw accuracy\\n    # dosage>0 models: d=0 mean as counterfactual reference\\n    ground_truth = np.full(len(dosages), gt_acc_d0)\\n    ground_truth[d0_mask] = raw_acc[d0_mask]\\n\\n    return ground_truth\\n\\n\\ndef run_pipeline(args):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run full exp-006 pipeline.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.4}}"
    }
```
```json
  },
```
